# Alphonso — Claude Code Guide

## ALWAYS READ FIRST

`docs/ALPHONSO_GROUND_TRUTH.md` — verified facts about what exists in this repo. Do not trust any audit report or summary document that contradicts it. Past audits contained significant errors (see "Known Audit Errors" section in that file).

`docs/TRUTH_FIRST_EXECUTION_PLAN.md` — the mandatory active work queue. Before
starting maintenance, security, dependency, release, agent-contract, iOS, voice,
or documentation work, select/update a task and follow its evidence rules.

---

## Build Commands

```bash
npm run dev              # Vite dev server only (port 5173)
npm run tauri dev        # Full Tauri dev with Rust backend (kill port 5173 first if busy)
npm run test             # Run all tests (src + bridge) — all should pass
npm run test:watch       # Watch mode
npm run build            # Web build only (no Tauri/Rust)
npm run verify:app       # lint + typecheck + test + build in one command
npm run lint             # ESLint on src/
npm run typecheck        # tsc --noEmit — full TypeScript check (0 errors as of v2.5.0)

# Rust (run from src-tauri/ directory)
cargo check              # Verify Rust compiles
cargo test               # Run Rust unit tests
cargo fmt --all -- --check   # Format check (rustfmt.toml sets tab_spaces=2)
cargo clippy -- -D warnings  # Lint Rust — must be zero warnings (CI enforces this)

# Updater / release
npm run release:updater  # One-command release pipeline (NSIS + MSI + signed manifest)
npm run updater:keygen   # Generate Tauri updater signing keys
npm run updater:verify   # Verify updater readiness

# Auth helpers
npm run auth:youtube     # OAuth flow for YouTube
npm run auth:meta        # OAuth flow for Meta/Instagram

# Coverage (actual measured 2026-09-05: lines 57.81%, branches 45.62%, functions 50.32%, statements 55.65%; enforced floors: lines 48%/branches 38%/functions 30%/statements 48%, scoped to src/)
npm run test:coverage    # Run tests with coverage report

# E2E — Playwright installed (no extra install needed)
# Requires: npm run dev running on :5173 + Ollama running with a model
npm run test:e2e         # Run Playwright golden-path smoke test (web build, Tauri bridge MOCKED)

# Desktop E2E — the real built app via tauri-driver, isolated test data (45 spec files, 129 checks)
# Requires: tauri-driver, an Edge driver matching WebView2 (ALPHONSO_E2E_EDGEDRIVER), and
#           npm run tauri build -- --debug --no-bundle   (see e2e-desktop/wdio.conf.mjs)
npm run test:e2e:desktop
```

## Key Architecture Facts

- **9 agents**: Alphonso, Jose, Hector, Miya, Maria, Marcus, Echo, Sentinel, Nova — all in `src/agents/`, all enforced by `agentContractService.ts`
- **policyEnforcementService.ts gates every outbound connector call**. Default fail-safe posture: missing credentials always block; Zero-Cost Mode (`zeroCostMode: true` by default) blocks paid/metered connectors; Approval Mode (`approvalMode: true` by default, changed from `false` in the 2026-08-17 security pass) blocks high-risk actions and high-risk connectors on fresh installs until the user explicitly approves or disables Approval Mode in Settings. To allow high-risk actions without per-action approval, the user must explicitly set `approvalMode: false` in Settings. Verify current defaults in `src/services/policyEnforcementService.ts` before relying on this in security-sensitive work.
- **licenseService.ts**: license tier validation (Free/Pro/Enterprise) for premium connectors (`PREMIUM_CONNECTORS`: claude, chatgpt, youtube, notion, clickup, whatsapp, sd_webui, comfyui). **Launching free (2026-09-30):** gating is inert while `LICENSE_TRUST_KEY` in `src/config/licenseTrustKey.ts` is `null` — `isPremiumGatingEnabled()` returns false and every connector is usable. Pasting a real vendor public JWK re-enables paid tiers with no other change. An approval no longer bypasses the license check (`policyEnforcementService.ts`).
- **parallelExecutionService.ts**: parallel task execution with concurrency control, retry logic, and task queues
- **cacheService.ts**: memory caching with TTL, LRU eviction, and global/connector/agent caches
- **34 connectors** (`DEFAULT_CONNECTORS` in `connectorRegistry.js`; ElevenLabs and Higgsfield added in content factory phase 4b, both paid and high risk; Google Web Risk in agent phase 4): Telegram, WhatsApp, YouTube, Meta (Facebook and Instagram, content factory phase 3a: keys saved in Settings reach `meta_publish_content` through `savedMetaCredentials()` in `metaPublishService.ts` and `resolve_meta_credentials()` in `meta_publish.rs`; Page posts use `META_PAGE_ACCESS_TOKEN` when set; a video on this computer is posted as a Reel by `meta_publish_video` in `meta_video.rs` / `publishMetaVideo()`: Meta's resumable upload (`rupload.facebook.com`), Instagram now (Meta can't schedule it), Facebook now or scheduled on Meta (`facebook_schedule()`: under 11 minutes ahead posts now, over 29 days is refused), then waits up to 10 minutes for Meta's own status and returns `posted` / `scheduled` / `failed` / `setup_required`), mobile_bridge, ChatGPT, Claude, Qwen, Notion, ClickUp, SD WebUI, ComfyUI Video, Runway, GitHub, Slack, Discord, Generic Webhook, Ollama, Brave Search, Perplexity, Tavily, DeepSeek, n8n, NVIDIA NIM, Gemini, Hermes Agents, CALL-E, the free stock footage sources Pexels, Pixabay and Coverr (content factory phase 2a), and Your storage (`media_storage`, phase 3c: the user's own optional S3-compatible bucket, used only inside an already-approved post, so it has no gate of its own), and Google Web Risk (`web_risk`, agent phase 4: an optional check of links against Google's phishing and malware lists, free for the first 100,000 checks a month, so not paid) — all policy-gated, all registered centrally, all have credential input UI in ConnectorSetupPanel. NVIDIA NIM, Gemini, and Hermes Agents are deliberately excluded from `PAID_OR_METERED_CONNECTORS` in `policyEnforcementService.ts` (NVIDIA/Gemini: free-tier cloud, see `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-23-free-tier-cloud-providers.md`; Hermes Agents: local/self-hosted, same posture as Ollama). CALL-E is registered as paid/high-risk (real outbound phone calls).
- **lib.rs is ~948 lines** — 52 Rust source files in src-tauri/src/, 197 Tauri commands, 368 Rust `#[test]`s (384 with the async ones) (as of 2026-10-25; `verify-doc-counts.mjs` holds the live numbers) — see AGENTS.md for the module list
- **6,916 unit tests across 596 test files** (as of 2026-10-28, local full run on `fix/qa-batch-0`; `src/test/` plus `bridge/tests/`); Vitest via vitest.config.js (separate from vite build config)
- **Security (Batch 1 complete)**: boot TDZ crash fixed; `gateConnectorAction` exception-safe + DSL-wired; SSRF blocking on `fetch_url_content`; `execute_command_verified` real output redaction; PKCE on all 3 OAuth scripts; clipboard/dialog/open_url use native APIs (arboard, tauri-plugin-dialog, tauri-plugin-opener); CSP narrowed to explicit ports; per-program arg allowlist in `policy_gate.rs` (tightened 2026-09-30: every allowed program has an explicit fail-closed argument rule; `node -e`, `python -c`, `npx <pkg>`, `npm publish`, git `--upload-pack`/`-c`/`ext::`, and curl/wget/docker/URL openers are refused — LLM-planned commands from `agentBrainService.js` reach this path, so it is the real boundary).
- **CI workflows**: `ci.yml` (lint + test + build + Tauri artifact + cargo test/clippy + npm audit + cargo audit + desktop E2E + Tauri crate/npm version check), `release.yml` (tag-triggered build + sign + publish), `gate.yml` (governance gate), `ios-build.yml`, `deploy-cloud-voice.yml`, and `security-audit.yml` (weekly npm + cargo audit, added 2026-10-21). The cargo-audit ignore list lives only in `.cargo/audit.toml`; `scripts/cargo-audit.sh` reads it for both CI and the weekly run, so do not add `--ignore` flags anywhere else.
- **`.npmrc`** has `legacy-peer-deps=true` — required because `@eslint/js@10` and `eslint@9` have a peer dep mismatch. Do not remove.
- **`eslint.config.js` covers `.ts`/`.tsx` as of 2026-07-02** (`typescript-eslint` parser/plugin, `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` block) — previously it only covered `.js`/`.jsx` and every ".ts/.tsx" file in the repo went completely unlinted. A dedicated override block (by exact file path, not a wildcard) disables `@typescript-eslint/ban-ts-comment` for the 9 pre-existing `@ts-nocheck` files (now resolved — those component files no longer carry `@ts-nocheck`); do not widen that override list; fix `@ts-nocheck` at the source for any new file instead.
- **Multi-turn Ollama**: `generateOllamaChatStream` in `src/lib/ollama.ts` uses `/api/chat` — full conversation history is passed per message. `ChatView.tsx` captures history snapshot before React state updates. When the desktop window is refused (HTTP 403 from a user-started Ollama, CORS, not running, timeout) it falls back to the `ollama_chat` Tauri command, which sends the same turns to `/api/chat` from Rust with one bounded retry (added 2026-10-21; before that the fallback flattened the chat into one `ollama_generate` prompt). The reply then arrives in one piece, not streamed. A thinking model's reasoning (Ollama's `message.thinking`, or `<think>...</think>` from older versions, split by `src/lib/thinking.ts`) never goes into the answer: `generateOllamaChatStream`'s `onThinking` and the backend proof's `thinking` field carry it, and ChatView shows it folded as "Thinking" unless `settings.showModelThinking === false` (Settings → Ollama Runtime; owner, 2026-10-22). Do not send Ollama's `think` option: a model without thinking refuses the request. Pulling a model: `pullOllamaModel()` uses the backend's `ollama_pull_model` (progress as `ollama://pull_progress` events with a `pullId`) inside the desktop app, since the window is refused by an Ollama the user started; the Chat picker and Setup both call it (2026-10-22). Do NOT fetch `/api/pull` from a component.
- **appendAgentActivity**: wired in `joseExecutionEngineService.ts` (`executeAssignment`) and `connectorRegistryService.ts` (`appendConnectorAudit`). Activity tab now shows live data.
- **CREATE_NO_WINDOW on all Windows process spawns**: a shared `no_window()`/`no_window_async()` helper pair in `utils.rs` (`pub(crate) fn no_window(cmd: &mut std::process::Command)` / `no_window_async(cmd: &mut tokio::process::Command)`) must be applied to every `std::process::Command::new()`/`TokioCommand::new()` call site on Windows, or a visible CMD window flashes open/closed for that spawn. As of 2026-07-10 this is applied everywhere in `lib.rs`, `plugin_runtime.rs`, `voice_sidecar.rs`, `workspace.rs`, and `runtime_manager.rs` (incl. the polled `kill_pid`/`is_pid_alive` health-check helpers, which were the most likely source of *recurring* flashes since they run on every process-alive check). **CI-enforced as of 2026-09-02 (closes T14's lint half):** `scripts/verify-no-window-coverage.mjs` (`npm run verify:no-window-coverage`) scans every `.rs` file under `src-tauri/src` for an unguarded spawn site and fails the `Rust Tests & Clippy` CI job (blocking, not advisory) if one is found — a bare `Command::new("kill"|"ps"|"which")` literal is exempt since those are POSIX-only tools the flag can't apply to. `workspace.rs`'s whisper-transcription spawn was migrated off a raw inline `creation_flags(0x0800_0000)` to the shared `no_window_async()` helper as part of this pass, so the lint has one consistent pattern to check for. Apply the helper to any new spawn site — the lint will now catch it if you forget, instead of relying on memory.
- **Port map for local services** (collision caused a real P0 — see Companion/Voice OS rows below): Ollama 11434, Alphonso Bridge 4444, MCP server 3333, ComfyUI 8188, n8n 5678, Mobile Companion WebSocket **8765** (hardcoded in iOS Swift, do not change), Voice OS **8766** (moved off 8765 on 2026-07-10 due to the collision), AudioCraft 7866, Open WebUI 3001, and the in-app sign-in callbacks (content factory phase 3c, `127.0.0.1` only, open for 5 minutes during a sign-in): YouTube 8085, Meta 8086, Instagram 8087. Check this list before adding any new local service's default port.

---

## Do Not Duplicate — These Already Exist

Before writing any new service, component, or feature, check this list:

The source-backed inventory is generated at [`docs/DO_NOT_DUPLICATE.generated.md`](docs/DO_NOT_DUPLICATE.generated.md). Refresh it with `npm run generate:dnd-map`; CI runs `npm run verify:dnd-map` so new services/components and their static exports cannot drift out of the map. This generated inventory complements the human purpose descriptions below; it does not replace them.

| Thing you might think is missing | Where it actually lives |
|---|---|
| Connector health UI (full panel) | `src/components/ConnectorHealthPanel.tsx` (lazy chunk) |
| Connector status dot/strip for sidebars | `src/components/ConnectorStatusIndicators.tsx` — import from HERE not ConnectorHealthPanel |
| Connector status classification (live/missing_config/foundation_only/placeholder/disabled) | `src/services/connectorStatusService.ts` — `deriveConnectorStatus()`. Both `ConnectorStatusIndicators.tsx` and `ConnectorHealthPanel.tsx` import this one function; do NOT redefine a local `deriveStatus()` in a new component — that duplication is exactly what caused a real connector-count drift bug (fixed 2026-08-22, see `docs/governance/DEFERRED_WORK.md`) |
| Approval modal with risk levels | `src/components/ApprovalModal.tsx` |
| Approval panel (shared, props-driven) | `src/components/ApprovalPanel.tsx` — generalized approve/reject UI over injected `onApprove`/`onReject`/`getItemDetail` props, keyed on a normalized `itemId` (not a hardcoded `agentBusService` import). Two real callers: `ChatView.tsx` (wraps `approvePacket`/`rejectPacket`/`getPacketById` from `agentBusService`) and `ProjectExecutionMode.tsx`'s Approval tab (wraps `approveRequest`/`rejectRequest` from `services/approval/approvalService.js`). Distinct from `ApprovalModal.tsx` (risk-level modal) and `src/components/approval/ApprovalCenterPanel.tsx` (a separate, older read-only pending list, superseded for gating purposes by the Approval tab but left in place — see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-project-execution-approval-gate-design.md`) |
| Toast notifications | `ToastProvider.tsx` in `main.jsx`, inbound toasts in `App.tsx` |
| Policy / approval enforcement | `src/services/policyEnforcementService.ts` |
| License tier validation | `src/services/licenseService.ts` |
| Parallel execution + retry | `src/services/parallelExecutionService.ts` |
| Memory caching (TTL + LRU) | `src/services/cacheService.ts` |
| Orchestration queue + dead-letter | `src/services/orchestrationQueueService.ts` |
| Receipt / audit events | `src/services/orchestrationReceiptService.ts` |
| Zero-cost mode logic | `policyEnforcementService.ts` + Jose routing |
| Agent contract boundaries | `src/services/agentContractService.ts` — `AGENT_EXECUTION_CONTRACTS` is built from the one permission table (row below) since 2026-10-23. `validateAgentExecutionContract()` checks a packet's declared `effects` against the table; only a packet without effects (older code, Boardroom bridge, Project Execution) still gets the description-keyword check. The skill-pack exception for running code reads the table (`isEffectAllowed(agent, 'run')`), not a hardcoded Alphonso |
| One permission table for every agent | `src/services/jose/agentPermissionTable.ts` (Jose redesign phase 1, 2026-10-23; one owner per kind of work since agent roles Part A, 2026-10-24: no task-type prefix belongs to two agents (`agent_report` aside), each row has `owns` and `never` sentences, `ownerOf(taskType)`, `describeResponsibility(agent)`, `responsibilityLines()`; `src/test/services/jose/agentOwnership.test.ts` fails on an overlap or an unowned action type. Jose owns planning, routing, approvals and the closing summary only; Alphonso code, commands, plain questions and everyday writing (`assistant_`); Miya content and design (`creative_`, `design_`, incl. `creative_assemble_video`), never code; Hector finding facts; Nova judging what Hector found; Maria quality, governance and risk (`risk_`); Sentinel security; Marcus sending, publishing and calls (`outreach_`, `distribution_readiness`); Echo memory. The planner is told each agent's `owns`/`never` and checks task types) — `AGENT_PERMISSION_TABLE` (per agent: `role`, `taskTypes`, `blockedTaskTypes`, `effects`), `isTaskTypeAllowed()`, `isEffectAllowed()`, `describeAgentPermissions()` (plain lines for the screens). Only Alphonso may `run`/`change_project`; only Marcus may `publish_or_send`; everyone may `read` and `write_own_work` (new files in the project folder). Sentinel audits security (`security_`), Maria audits quality and governance (`audit_`). Shown on the Hector desk. The 9 `src/agents/*/…Permissions.js` files are a descriptive vocabulary only, never enforced. Do NOT add a second permission list |
| Effects and approval by effect | `src/services/jose/joseEffects.ts` — `JoseEffect` (`read`, `write_own_work`, `change_project`, `run`, `spend`, `publish_or_send`, `delete`), `LOCKED_EFFECTS` (spend, publish, delete: always ask, even with Approval Mode off), `DEFAULT_EFFECT_APPROVALS`, `getEffectApprovals()` (Settings `effectApprovals`), `effectsNeedingApproval()`, `stepEffectsToWaitFor()` (what a whole step waits on: `run` is asked per command instead, `COMMAND_CHECKED_EFFECTS`), `ACTION_EFFECTS` + `inferEffects(actionType)` (unknown → `run`, fail closed). The bus (`canExecutePacket`), the router (`requiresApproval`) and the pipeline's effect gate all use it. Add a new task type to `ACTION_EFFECTS` |
| Jose plans as step graphs | `src/services/jose/stepGraph.ts` — `PlanStep` (`stepId`, `agent`, `actionType`, `effects`, `needs`), `JOSE_LIMITS` (50 steps, 30 min normal, 2 h project, 2 fix rounds), `checkPlan(steps, { taskTypes })` (cycles, missing or duplicate steps, unknown agents, run only by Alphonso, publish only by Marcus, task types per the table), `buildStepWaves()`, `legacyNeeds()` (the splitter's old fixed order, from `jose/agentOrder.ts`'s `AGENT_DEPENDENCIES`). `createJoseCommandRoute` gives every assignment a `stepId` (agent, then `agent-2`, …), `effects` and `needs`; the pipeline refuses a bad plan before anything runs (`reason: 'plan_rejected'`, `problems`). In an explicit plan a step runs only once every step it needs is done (`not_run` otherwise); the splitter's free-text plans still run a step after an earlier one failed, as before. Since phase 4 the pipeline runs every ready step, then asks `adaptPlan()` (below) what to change, and again until nothing is left: added steps are checked with `checkPlan()` and the 50-step limit before they are added (refused ones get a "Not added: …" note), get packets through `createStepPacket()` and are saved on the command (`appendCommandAssignments()`). A step can carry `model`, `reviewOf`, `round`, `revises`, `helpRound`, `replaces` and `costEstimateUsd`; a step with a paid model (`claude:`/`chatgpt:`) may spend, since that always asks |
| Saved Jose requests and step outputs | `src/services/jose/joseRequestStore.ts` (`alphonso_jose_requests_v1`, 200 kept: `createJoseRequest`, `updateJoseStep`, `setJoseRequestStatus`, `getJoseRequest`, `listJoseRequests`, `markRunningRequestsInterrupted`; phase 4: `addRequestSteps`, `rewireRequestNeeds`, `appendRequestNote`, `findRequestByPacket(packetId)`; agent phase 6: `requestForCommand(commandId)`, the request a command runs on, including a plan you approved first, which keeps its own `jose-request-plan-…` id: use it, never a derived `jose-request-<commandId>`, a step's `verdict`) and `src/services/jose/joseStepOutputs.ts` (`alphonso_jose_step_outputs_v1`, the last 50 commands plus every command whose request is still waiting, waiting for its plan, interrupted or running (phase 6a, so work approved days later is still there): `setStepOutput`, `getStepOutput`, `priorOutputsForNeeds`). A step's `priorOutputs` are the outputs of the steps it needs, keyed by agent; `agentOutputStoreService` still keeps each agent's latest step for callers that read by agent |
| Jose templates | `src/services/jose/joseTemplates.ts` (phase 2, 2026-10-23) — `JOSE_TEMPLATES` (Content video, Build an app (`project`), Research report), each with `matchWords` groups and steps with an `instruction`; `scoreTemplateFit(text)` (matched groups ÷ groups, whole words), `TEMPLATE_FIT_THRESHOLD` (0.75: every group must match), `getTemplate()`. Templates are starting points, never required |
| Jose's planner | `src/services/jose/josePlanner.ts` — `planJoseRequest(text, { planFresh, history, signal })` → `JosePlan` (`steps`, `source`: `template` / `adapted` / `fresh` / `rules`, `templateId`, `kind`, `note`); `describePlanSource()` ("Planned fresh.", "Based on template X (adapted).", or the fallback note). Jose's model (`generateAgentLlmResponse('jose', …)`) writes the plan, offered only the best-fitting template ("Starting point"); declared effects are unioned with `inferEffects()` so a step cannot dodge approval; `checkPlan()` checks it; one fix with the problems listed; then a fitting template unchanged or the rule splitter (`decomposeJoseCommand`), with a note saying why (owner, 2026-10-23). Since agent phase 5 the example is a real plan (`PLAN_EXAMPLE`), and copied placeholders (`copiedPlaceholders()`) and anything the request explicitly asks for but the plan leaves out (a send, a readiness check, a check of the writing, a plan review: `missingAsked()`; since agent phase 6 also a code request without Alphonso's code step, and plain writing planned with Hector's research) are named in the fix round. Tests prove he plans fresh when no template fits and with "plan this fresh" Since Jose phase 7 the request comes first and the chat history is background only (`HISTORY_HEADING`); `historyOnlyDestinations()` sends back a plan using a destination only the history named. `planBatch(texts)` plans a whole batch in one call (20 tasks a call, `BATCH_LABEL`), each plan checked; the batch orchestrator runs planned tasks through `submitStepsToJose()` and the rest through `answerOrPlan()`. Since QA batch 0 `missingAsked()` also sends back a file change in a saved project without Alphonso's code step (`asksForProjectWork()` in `alphonso/projects.ts`, which the rule plan uses too) and a "which first" request without Nova (`ASKS_WHICH_FIRST`). Since QA batch 1 `copiedPlaceholders(steps, request)` names a step copying `PLAN_EXAMPLE`'s instruction, and `missingAsked()` sends back a writing request without anyone who writes. |
| Answer directly or plan | `src/services/jose/joseDecision.ts` — `decideJoseRoute(text, { history })` → `{ route, source, reason }`: an explicit `/jose` plans and a plain question (`isPlainQuestion()` in `chatUtils.js`) is answered, both without a model call; anything else is decided by Jose's model, cut off after `DECISION_TIMEOUT_MS` (20 s); on error or timeout `shouldRouteThroughJose()` decides Since QA batch 0 a message asking for two or more kinds of work (research, writing, a check, a send, code: `kindsOfWork()` over `parseJoseCommand()`) is planned by rule. |
| `submitToJose()` (Jose's entry point) | `src/services/jose/submitToJose.ts` — `submitToJose({ text, source, history, planFresh, … })`: `runPipelineShortcuts()` first (image request → ComfyUI, named workflow), then `planJoseRequest()`; a rules plan runs `runJoseCommandExecutionPipeline` without assignments (the old path), any other plan as explicit steps with `requestKind` and `planInfo`. Returns the pipeline result plus `plan` and `planNote` (with `CONTENT_VIDEO_STUDIO_NOTE` for a content video). Chat uses it; since phase 6a every other caller reaches it through `answerOrPlan()` (or `submitStepsToJose()` for given steps) |
| Messages from anywhere; given steps (phase 6a) | `src/services/jose/joseInbound.ts` — `answerOrPlan(text, { source, history?, endpoint?, zeroCostMode?, showPlanFirst?, allowPaidModels?, onProgress? })`: `decideJoseRoute()`, then a direct answer from Alphonso's model (`CHAT_ASSISTANT_PROMPT`; empty reply → error) or `submitToJose()`. `submitStepsToJose({ text, source, steps, label, … })` in `submitToJose.ts` runs steps a caller already decided (Boardroom, workflows, batch) as a request: `checkPlan` with task types first, `plan.source = 'given'`, the label as the note. `allowPaidModels: false` (schedules) reaches the planner (no paid model offered), the run and `adaptPlan` (no paid retry), and is saved in the request's `runOptions` so a resume keeps it. `background: true` returns `{ kind: 'started', done }` at once; `inboundReply()` is what a channel says back, `outcomeLine()` one line for how a request ended. Callers so far: the Telegram companion (free text, `/ask`, `/research` asked by name), the WhatsApp companion (sends `outcomeLine()` when the request ends), `telegramAutoPollService.ts`, and the pollers through `sendConnectorMessageToJose()` in `connectorPolling.js` (an answer is kept on the packet as `answered`). Do NOT call `createJoseCommandRoute` / `runJoseCommandExecutionPipeline` from a new caller |
| Phone companion commands (phase 6a) | `src/services/companionCommandService.ts` — `handleCompanionCommand(command, { broadcast, endpoint, model, zeroCostMode, signal })`, called from App.tsx's `companion://command` listener: a spoken conversation (`voiceConversation`) is a direct reply from the chosen agent, as before; anything else goes through `answerOrPlan()` with progress as `agent_status` events. The `done` summary is the answer, the request's `userReport.summary`, or `outcomeLine()` when a plan or step waits for you. Nothing is sent after an abort |
| A schedule's command (phase 6a) | `src/services/scheduledCommandService.ts` — `runScheduledCommand(text, { endpoint, zeroCostMode })`, App.tsx's `runCommand` for `startScheduler()`: `answerOrPlan()` with `showPlanFirst: 'never'` and `allowPaidModels: false` (owner, 2026-10-23), mapped to the scheduler's `{ ok, summary, details, pendingApprovalCount, error }`. A step that needs approval still waits and Telegram asks |
| Boardroom thread actions as Jose requests (phase 6a) | `src/services/boardroomJoseService.ts` — `concludeOnJose(topic, transcript)` (one Maria `audit_governance` step, `read`; returns her `review` from the step output's `schema`), `distributeOnJose(topic, summary, mode, channel)` (Marcus `distribution_slack` with `publish_or_send` and `payload: { channel, text }`, so it waits for approval; or `distribution_review`, `read`), `creativeBriefOnJose(topic, context)` (Miya `creative_brief`). All through `submitStepsToJose()` with `BOARDROOM_LABEL` ("Steps set by Boardroom."). `PlanStep.payload` carries a given step's data to its agent; the engine keeps an agent's `schema` in the step output. Agent replies in a thread stay direct chat (owner) |
| What a step produced (board, phase 6a, QA Bug 4) | `src/components/jose/JoseStepOutput.tsx` — `JoseStepOutput({ commandId, stepId })` over `getStepOutput()`: the summary, files written (from artifacts with `path`/`relativePath`/`filePath`), sources (opened through `openExternalUrl()`, not `target="_blank"`), and the step's `schema` (or an artifact's `data`) as text through `toReadableText()`, cut at 2,000 characters with Show all; "Nothing was saved for this step." otherwise. `JoseRequestsBoard.tsx` shows it under the map for the step you pick (`onSelectStep`) |
| Fixed agent order (splitter only) | `src/services/jose/agentOrder.ts` — `AGENT_DEPENDENCIES`, re-exported by `agentOutputStoreService.ts`. Used only to give the rule-based splitter's tasks their `needs`; `buildExecutionPlan()` is no longer used by the pipeline |
| Showing Jose's plan first | `src/services/jose/josePlanApproval.ts` (phase 3, 2026-10-23) — `getPlanFirstSetting()` (`settings.showPlanFirst`: `always` / `bigger` (default) / `never`) and `needsPlanApproval(steps, setting)`: bigger = 4 or more agents counting Jose and Maria, or any step with a locked effect (owner). `submitToJose()` then saves the plan with `createWaitingPlanRequest()` (status `waiting_plan_approval`, `runOptions`, `changeLog`) and returns `{ waitingForPlan, requestId }`; `runApprovedPlan(requestId)` claims it once (`claimPlanForRun()`), re-runs `checkPlan()` (problems → back to waiting via `returnPlanToWaiting()`), and runs it on the same request (`existingRequestId` → `attachRunToRequest()`); `cancelPlan()`. A waiting plan is not marked interrupted at start |
| Editing a plan; user templates | `src/services/jose/josePlanEdits.ts` — pure `removeStep()` (also from others' `needs`), `addStep()` (unique `<agent>-<n>` id, effects inferred), `changeStepAgent()`, `setStepNeeds()`, `setStepInstruction()`; the caller runs `checkPlan()`. `src/services/jose/joseUserTemplates.ts` — `saveUserTemplate()` (id `user_<slug>`, `-2`… when taken; `matchWords` from the name and the request), `listUserTemplates()`, `deleteUserTemplate()` (`alphonso_jose_user_templates_v1`); `joseTemplates.ts`'s `allTemplates()` makes `scoreTemplateFit()`/`getTemplate()` treat them like the built-in ones |
| Plan map layout | `src/services/jose/josePlanLayout.ts` — `layoutPlan(steps, box)`: columns from `buildStepWaves()`, rows within a wave, edges from `needs`; a plan that breaks the rules is drawn as a list without arrows. No graph library (plans are layered by construction) |
| A model per step; larger when a step keeps failing | `src/services/jose/joseModels.ts` (phase 4, 2026-10-23) — `modelSize()`, `pickLargerModel()`, `listInstalledModels()` (Settings address). `withAgentModel(agentId, model, fn)` / `getAgentModelOverride()` in `src/lib/ollama.ts` give one step its model; the engine's `executeAssignmentWithRetries` runs a step with `assignment.model`, and after the retries tries the next larger installed model once (Ollama agents only), saying so in the failure and the receipt (`escalatedTo`). The planner offers installed models only |
| Jose changing a plan while it runs | `src/services/jose/joseAdapt.ts` — `adaptPlan(ctx)` returns `PlanChange[]` (`add`, `rewire`, `reason`, `verdict`); `setPlanAdapterForTests()`. It runs, in order: review loops (`joseReviewLoop.ts`: `judgeReview()` uses the review's own verdict, a content quality review's from its scores, else asks the reviewer's own model for `pass`/`fix`; unreadable → fix ("The review could not be read: check it on the board.", agent phase 3); `planReviewRound()` adds `<step>-fix-<n>` and `<review>-r<n>`, at most `JOSE_LIMITS.maxFixRounds` (2) rounds, then a note asking you), help requests (`joseHelp.ts`: a step ending "HELP: …" gets a helper (`chooseHelper()`: Jose's model, else the rules; read / own-work effects only, never the asking agent) and `<step>-help` + `<step>-again`, once per step; every planned non-Alphonso step is told about the HELP line), and paid retries (a step still failing after the local retries gets `<step>-paid`, with `replaces` and `costEstimateUsd`, when paid models are available). `rewireNeeds()` (in `josePlanEdits.ts`) moves later steps to the new ones. Do NOT change a running plan any other way Since Jose phase 7 a content-factory step's help re-run keeps its `contentStep` and gets the helper's answer ("Help from <Agent>:"), and content prompts offer `"help"` (`CONTENT_HELP_LINE`). |
| Requests away from the app (phase 5) | Interrupted requests: `markRunningRequestsInterrupted()` runs during App's first render (once; never in the Coach window, which shares storage), and `carryOnRequest(requestId)` (engine) resumes one — steps done are kept, a step that needs approval asks again; "Carry on" on the board, in Chat's notice (`data-testid="chat-interrupted-notice"`) and on Telegram. `rejectRequestStep(requestId, stepId)` rejects a waiting step and cancels the request ("You rejected <Agent>'s step."; board "Reject", Telegram Reject, `/reject`). Telegram: `handleJoseButton()` in `telegramCompanionService.js` (paired chat only; a stale token → "That button has expired."; work claimed at once so a second press says "Already done."), the push watcher sends `collectJoseNotifications()` with buttons and skips the old "Approval needed" for a request's packet, `/approve` and `/reject` go through the request. Backend: `connector_send_telegram(…, buttons?)` (inline keyboard; data ≤ 64 bytes checked by `telegram_send_payload()`), polling asks for `message` and `callback_query` (`parse_telegram_update()`; `ConnectorInboundMessage` has `callbackId`/`callbackData`), `connector_answer_telegram_callback`. Live-checked with the owner's bot on 2026-10-23 (`jose-telegram-live.e2e.mjs`, run by hand with `ALPHONSO_LIVE_TELEGRAM_TOKEN` / `ALPHONSO_LIVE_TELEGRAM_OWNER_CREDENTIAL`; skipped otherwise; values typed into Settings, never in the app's environment or the repo) |
| A request carries on after an approval | `resumeJoseRequest(requestId)` in `joseExecutionEngineService.ts` (phase 4): only a request that is `waiting_approval`, claimed as `running` before anything awaits (approving twice runs once); runs the same pipeline on the saved command and packets, done steps kept. `executeApprovedPackets()` sends a packet of a saved request there (all of one request's packets approved, then one resume); other packets keep the one-packet path. `approveRequestStep(requestId, stepId)` is the board's "Approve and carry on"; the Orchestrator's Execute on an approved request packet goes the same way. A step you approved keeps `approved` (the loop no longer re-queues it). The Settings Ollama address is used when none is passed |
| Stopping a running request (Jose phase 7) | `src/services/jose/joseStop.ts` — `stopRequest(requestId)` (a request `running` or `queued`; else `NOT_RUNNING`), `STOPPED_LINE` ("Stopped by you."), `stopSignal()` / `isStopRequested()` / `clearStop()`, `onStopRequested()` (the queue lets a waiting request go), `stopWaitsFor(assignment, packet)` (a Marcus send or an approved Alphonso code change finishes its one action first). The pipeline checks before every wave and step, runs each step inside `withAgentSignal()` (`src/lib/agentSignals.ts`, the same pattern as `withAgentModel`; `generateAgentLlmResponse` uses the step's signal) so the running model call is cancelled, never retries a stopped step, then marks the running step `stopped`, the rest `not_run`, the request `stopped` (final: Carry on refuses it), rejects its waiting packets and frees its projects (`releaseProjectsHeldBy()` in `projectLock.ts`). Screens: the board's Stop (`jose-stop`), Chat's live line (`chat-live-stop`), Telegram `/stop` (`handleStopCommand()`, the newest running request); `closingSummary()` starts "Stopped by you.". `cancelRequest(requestId)` in the engine cancels a request `interrupted` or `waiting_setup` (`CANCELLED_LINE`; board `jose-cancel-request`, Chat's notice `chat-notice-cancel-<id>`, Telegram's Cancel beside Carry on, `cancel_request`). Do NOT stop a request any other way |
| One local-model request at a time (Jose phase 7) | `src/services/jose/joseQueue.ts` — `usesLocalModel(steps)` (any step not on a paid model or a NVIDIA/Gemini provider; any content step), `takeTurn(requestId)` (status `queued` with `queuedAtMs` while waiting), `giveBackTurn()`, `leaveQueue()`, `queuePosition()`, `QUEUED_LINE(n)` ("Queued (2nd)."), `WAITING_FOR_TURN`, `requeueAtStart(carryOn)` (App's first render: queued requests carried on oldest first). The pipeline takes a turn after the plan is accepted; a request store listener (`onRequestStatusChange()` in `joseRequestStore.ts`) gives it back when the request stops running (done, failed, waiting for you, stopped), and `runJoseCommandExecutionPipeline()` is a wrapper whose `finally` gives it back however the run ends. Plain answers never queue. The board shows the place (`statusLabel()`), Chat the waiting line. Not the resource manager (nothing measures memory) |
| Hand-offs mid-step (Jose phase 7) | `src/services/jose/joseHandoff.ts` — `ACTION_INSTRUCTION` (told to every planned step except on a help round, beside `HELP_INSTRUCTION`), `findActionRequest(output)` (`ACTION: send …` / `ACTION: code …`, the first line), `handoffSteps(step, request, existing)` (`<step>-send` Marcus `distribution_execution` with `publish_or_send`, or `<step>-code` Alphonso `local_operation`; none when the agent owns the action, it exists already, or on a help round), `handoffReason()`. `adaptPlan()` adds them through `addSecurityChecks()` (Sentinel's check before a hand-off send or code change), and they wait for approval by effect. `ACTION_TOOL_REFUSAL` in `skillToolRun.ts` tells an agent calling an action tool to use the line |
| A project's requests on the board (Jose phase 7) | `src/services/jose/requestGroups.ts` — `groupRequests(requests)`: a project's requests together under its name where the newest falls, newest first; `JoseRequest.group` (`setRequestGroup()`), passed by `submitStepsToJose({ group })` and every content-factory run (`{ id: project.id, label: project.name }`); the board's heading `jose-group-<id>` |
| Echo saves a finished request | `src/services/jose/joseFinish.ts` — `saveRequestDecisions(request, outputs)`: since agent phase 7 Echo's model writes the decisions (`extractDecisions()`) when a request ends done or failed, and when you reject a step, in the background (the result never waits for it); skipped when the plan already has an Echo save step or was only his own memory work, and drafts (writing, creative, design) are left out of the work; never throws; "Echo remembered: …" per decision, "Echo found nothing new to remember." or "Echo couldn't save what was decided: …" in the change log |
| Echo's decisions (agent phase 7) | `src/services/echo/` (2026-10-26, owner: Echo is the brain of the whole ecosystem; every agent can get past decisions from him). `decisions.ts` — `Decision` (`kind` decision / preference / instruction / rejection, `text`, `why`, `topics`, `appliesTo`, `source { surface, requestId, stepIds, sources, by }`, `status` active / replaced / forgotten, `replaces` / `replacedBy`), `alphonso_echo_decisions_v1` (`durableSet`), `ECHO_LIMITS` (5,000 kept, 2,000 / 6,000 characters per step, 8 per step), `addDecision()` (keys hidden by `redactText()`; marks the one it `replaces` replaced), `editDecision()`, `forgetDecision()`, `replaceWith()`, `listDecisions()`, `activePreferences()`, `surfaceOf()`; graph nodes `decision` with `decided_in`, `cites` and `replaces` edges, and `decisionIds()` protects them from graph pruning. Decisions never expire. `recall.ts` — `searchDecisions()` (word stems, topics weigh more, plus ChromaDB when it runs), `decisionsFor(text, { agent, actionType, large })` (preferences that apply plus matches; what every step gets as `StepInput.memory`; ChromaDB joins once a background check, at most once a minute, finds it running: a search never waits for it), `decisionLine()`, `decisionsText()`, `answerDecisionsBlock()` (Chat's and channels' answers). `echoSteps.ts` — `runEchoStep()`: save (`extractDecisions()`: his model writes only decisions that are in the request or the work, one retry, a `replaces` id must be one he was shown; dropped: a copy of the work (60+ characters), a near-repeat of a saved one (`nearlySame()` in `recall.ts`, 75% of the meaning words, numbers always counted), the answer format's own hints; "Remember that" stripped (`withoutRememberWords()`); an unlinked new decision sharing words with saved ones gets one small "does it replace one of these?" question (`findReplaced()`); when you asked to remember and his model saves nothing twice, your own words are kept as an instruction; nothing → "Nothing to remember here."; failure → "Echo couldn't save …", nothing made up), recall (`memory_recall`), forget (`memory_forget`: one clear match is forgotten; several wait with `decisionChoices`), cleanup. `cleanup.ts` / `cleanupStore.ts` — `proposeCleanup()` (merge, replace, contradiction, drop, promote an old note; ids he wasn't shown dropped; the step waits with `cleanupSetId`; nothing changes until applied), `applyCleanup()`, `dismissCleanup()`, `askEchoCleanup()` (the Sunday schedule "Echo: tidy memory", `ECHO_CLEANUP_LABEL`). `echoPicks.ts` — `pickForget()`, `applyCleanupStep()`, `dismissCleanupStep()` (the board's and Telegram's buttons; a stale press → "Already done."). Routing: "Remember …", "Forget …" and "Clean up my memory" are planned by a rule (`asksToRemember()`, `asksToForget()`, `asksToCleanUpMemory()`, `asksWhatWasDecided()` in `src/services/echo/memoryIntents.ts`, re-exported by `joseCommandRouterService.ts`); one that asks for nothing else is one Echo step without Jose's model (`memoryOnlyTask()`); the planner sends back a plan without Echo's step, and a forget or cleanup step nobody asked for; a HELP line about what was decided goes to Echo. Echo's own recall, forget and cleanup are not saved again when the request ends (`joseFinish.ts`). Saved from every finished or failed request, approvals and rejections, kept lessons and Miya Studio rejections. Screens: `src/components/echo/EchoRemembered.tsx` ("Echo remembered: …" with Edit / Forget, in Chat and on the board), `DecisionsPanel.tsx` (Settings → Memory, replacing the old timeline), `CleanupProposals.tsx`; the board's forget buttons `echo-forget-<id>`. `echoMemoryService.ts` re-exports this API for older imports. Health-check and screen-observer notes are no longer saved (`removeCheckNotes()` clears the old ones once). Do NOT add a second decision store or save a note per request Since agent roles Part C2: `memory_digest` ("This week's decisions (n):" by topic, no model call) and meaning search (row below). |
| Echo by meaning (agent roles Part C2) | `src/services/echo/meaning.ts` — `ECHO_EMBED_MODEL` (`all-minilm`, 46 MB, pulled through `pullOllamaModel()` when missing), `ensureMeaning()` (once, on the first recall: the model, then every active decision's vector; never throws), `meaningState()` / `onMeaningState()` (`off`, `no_ollama`, `downloading`, `indexing`, `ready`, `failed`), `meaningMatches(query, pool, limit)` (cosine ≥ 0.55, waits at most 4 s, else none), `indexDecisionMeaning()` (a new decision once ready), `meaningEnabled()` (`settings.echoMeaningSearch !== false`). Vectors kept through `kv_set` as `echo_vec:<id>`. Backend `ollama_embed(endpoint, model, inputs)` in `ollama.rs` (`/api/embed`, at most 64 inputs of 8,000 characters). `searchDecisions()` in `recall.ts` adds its matches after the word matches. Screen: `src/components/echo/MeaningSearchStatus.tsx` (Settings → Memory, "Match decisions by meaning", `data-testid="echo-meaning-status"`). Word search works without it. Do NOT add a second embedding store Until QA batch 0 (2026-10-28) it read `http_status` from `ollama_list_models`, which sends `httpStatus`, so it never left "Needs Ollama running". Since QA batch 1 the cut-off is 0.45 (measured with the real `all-minilm`: right matches 0.38–0.71, wrong at most 0.44) and only the question's subject is embedded (`meaningQuestion()`). |
| What Jose sends to Telegram | `src/services/jose/joseTelegram.ts` (phase 5) — `collectJoseNotifications(requests, sent, settings)` (pure): a step waiting (what it will do in plain words, the cost for a paid step; Approve / Reject), a plan waiting (the steps; Run this plan / Cancel), finished or failed (one line), a review that asks you to decide, and always a request cut off when the app closed (Carry on). `getJoseTelegramSettings()` (`settings.joseTelegram`: the four Jose kinds on, and `activitySummaries` — the older "N agent actions since your last check" notifier in `runPushWatcher` — off unless switched on, owner 2026-10-23; the older "Jose completed a task" message is skipped for a command that belongs to a Jose request), the sent log (`markSent`/`wasSent`/`sentKeys`, `alphonso_jose_telegram_sent_v1`, 500 kept; `seedSentLog()` counts everything already there as sent the first time), `buttonToken()` / `readButtonToken()` (`j:` + 8 characters, `alphonso_jose_telegram_buttons_v1`, 200 kept, 7 days; Telegram allows 64 bytes). Settings → Approvals → "Jose on Telegram" (`JoseTelegramPanel`) |
| Work that changed owner (agent roles Part A) | `src/services/jose/movedTaskTypes.ts` — `MOVED_TASK_TYPES` (`runtime_assemble_video` → Miya `creative_assemble_video`, `orchestration_review` → Alphonso `assistant_answer`, `external_publish_handoff` → Marcus `distribution_readiness`, `creative_ui_design` → Miya `design_ui`), `moveToOwner()`, `dropRetiredSteps()` (Jose's `orchestration_summary` steps leave a plan; the closing summary replaces them), `toCurrentOwners()`. Used by the planner, `submitStepsToJose()`, `runApprovedPlan()` and a resumed request (packet and request step moved, with a change-log line) |
| One input for every step (agent roles Part A) | `src/services/jose/stepInput.ts` — `buildStepInput(commandId, assignment, request, { memory, large })` → `StepInput` (`instruction`, `request`, `needs` by step id with each needed step's full readable output, files and sources, `others` (one line per other finished step, at most 12 × 160 characters), `memory`, `review` = the `reviewOf` step's output), `STEP_INPUT_LIMITS` (6,000 characters per needed step, 20,000 for paid/cloud models; a cut says "(cut: N more characters on the board)"), `readableOutput()`, `stepInputText()`. Built once in the engine's `executeAssignment()` from the packet's `joseCommandId` and passed to every agent; Maria's, Nova's, Sentinel's and Echo's prompt builders take it, Hector researches with it, Marcus sends from it. `listStepOutputs(commandId)` in `joseStepOutputs.ts`. Until 2026-10-24 six agents got only the request text |
| Reviews judge the draft (agent roles Part A) | `src/services/jose/reviewStep.ts` — `runReviewStep(agent, stepInput, actionType)`: a non-content step with `reviewOf` reviews the reviewed step's output and answers `{ review, verdict, findings }` in one go (the verdict saved as the step's `schema`); nothing to review → fails "Nothing to review: …". `judgeReview()` reads that verdict and asks the model only when it is missing. Content-factory reviews keep their own executor |
| The closing summary (agent roles Part A) | `src/services/jose/joseSummary.ts` — `closingSummary(request)`, no model: one line per step ("Hector: <first sentence>", "waiting for you (…)", "needs setup (…)", "failed (…)", "not run"), "Waiting for you: N", "Open on the board."; a one-step request shows that step's full output (a review's hand-over only, without its data, since agent phase 4). `buildUserSummary()` in `joseCommandRouterService.ts` uses it for every command with a Jose request, so Chat, Telegram, WhatsApp, the phone and schedules show it |
| A step whose owner can't work (agent roles Part A) | An executor returns `resultState: 'setup_required'` with what to set up (Marcus's connector not set up; Miya's voice step when Kokoro isn't installed): tried once, never handed to another agent, step `setup_needed` with the reason, request `waiting_setup` (`JoseRequestStatus`); `carryOnRequest()` and `resumeJoseRequest()` accept it; the board shows Carry on, Telegram sends "<Agent> needs something set up: …" with Carry on (`collectJoseNotifications`), and it expires like a waiting request |

Chat's call-plan button and CALL-E Outreach's button say "Ask Marcus to place the call". The escalation service's calls (calling you about an approval) are unchanged |
| Voice agent roles (agent roles Part A) | `voice/backend/agent_roles.py` — `ROLES` (the same `owns`/`never` sentences as the permission table; `src/test/agentRolesVoiceSync.test.js` fails when they differ), `ROUTING_PATTERNS` (moved from `router.py`: emails and notes → Alphonso, timing and value → Nova, markets and competitors → Hector), `role_prompt(agent)`; `pipeline.build_messages()` sends it as the system message first |
| Agent display names | `src/services/jose/agentNames.ts` — `AGENT_NAMES`, `agentName(id)`. Use it; do NOT add another name map |
| Waiting requests expire | `src/services/jose/joseExpiry.ts` (phase 5) — `getWaitingExpiryDays()` (`settings.joseWaitingExpiryDays`: 7 by default, 0 or blank = never, negative → 7) and `expireWaitingRequests(nowMs)`: a request `waiting_approval` or `waiting_plan_approval` longer than that (by `updatedAtMs`) becomes `expired` with a note, and its `pending_approval` packets are rejected. Run once during App's first render and every hour (not in the Coach window). Settings → Approvals: "Waiting requests expire after (days)" |
| Paid models (Claude, ChatGPT) for Jose's steps | `src/services/jose/joseSpend.ts` (phase 4) — settings in `alphonso_settings.paidModels` (`allowed`, `provider`, `model`, prices per million input/output tokens, `monthlyCapUsd`), the month's total in `alphonso_jose_spend_v1`: `getPaidModelSettings()`, `paidModelAvailable()` (allowed, Zero-Cost Mode off, the connector set up, both prices entered; otherwise a plain reason), `paidModelId()`, `estimateCostUsd()` (characters ÷ 4), `recordSpend()`, `monthSpendUsd()`, `canSpend()` (the cap), `PAID_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`. `generateAgentLlmResponse` runs a step model `claude:<model>` / `chatgpt:<model>` through `sendClaudeMessage` / `sendChatGPTMessage`, refusing when not available or past the cap, recording the cost from the reported usage. Settings → Approvals → "Allow paid models" (`PaidModelsPanel` in `SettingsView.tsx`): off by default; the prices are yours to enter. A paid step has the `spend` effect, so it always asks, with "(about $X.XX)". Checked with unit tests only (no paid keys on the dev machine) |
| Plan map, board, editor and Chat card | `src/components/jose/`: `JosePlanMap.tsx` (boxes in `var(--agent-<id>)`, SVG arrows, "Waiting for you" badge, instruction cut with the full text on hover, `compact` for Chat), `JoseRequestsBoard.tsx` (the Orchestrator page's **Requests** tab: every request from `listJoseRequests()`, refreshed every 2 s; the selected one's map, how it was planned, change log; Run this plan / Edit / Cancel while waiting, "Approve and carry on" per step waiting for approval (phase 4), Save as template; the map shows "Round n" and a review's verdict), `JosePlanEditor.tsx` (remove/add a step, change agent, needs, instruction; live `checkPlan()` problems; Save changes disabled while the plan breaks the rules), `JosePlanCard.tsx` (the card in Chat: compact map, Run this plan / Edit on the board / Cancel). Chat's "Open on the board" goes through App's `onOpenJoseBoard` to `OrchestratorView`'s `initialTab="requests"` and `selectedRequestId`; since phase 4 it also shows on the live progress line from the pipeline's `request_started` event (`data-testid="chat-live-open-board"`) |
| Settings → Approvals table | `EffectApprovalsTable` in `src/components/SettingsView.tsx` (General, under Approval Mode) — a switch per effect (`Ask before: <label>`), saved as `settings.effectApprovals`; spend, publish and delete locked on ("Always asks"); unlocked rows greyed out while Approval Mode is off |
| 16 workflow operations | `src/services/workflowOperationsRegistryService.ts` |
| AI runtime manager (14 tools) | `src-tauri/src/runtime_manager.rs` + `src/services/runtimeManagerService.ts` + `src/components/RuntimeManagerView.tsx`. Install location (2026-10-22): the user chooses it (`InstallLocationPicker.tsx` in Runtime Hub and Setup; `runtime_get_install_location` / `runtime_set_install_location`, saved in `install_location.json` beside the old default); tools go in `<chosen>\Alphonso-runtimes` (`runtimes_dir()`), `tool_dir()` still finds a tool installed in the old default `%APPDATA%\Alphonso\runtimes` (`default_runtimes_dir()`), pip's cache is `<runtimes>\.pip-cache`, and the autostart prefs stay in the default folder. Never hardcode a location; use `tool_dir()`, not `runtimes_dir().join(name)`. `find_python()` (2026-10-22) picks a suitable Python (3.12 → 3.13 → 3.11 → 3.10 → 3.14, never below 3.10) from the PATH names, the Windows `py` launcher and the usual folders; it used to return the first `python` on PATH whatever its version. ComfyUI's install (2026-10-22) first installs PyTorch from the build matching the NVIDIA driver (`parse_nvidia_smi_cuda()` + `torch_index_for_cuda()`: CUDA 13+ → `cu130`, 12.6+ → `cu126`, else CPU with a "slow" note; macOS uses the default build), then `requirements.txt`, then downloads the Stable Diffusion 1.5 starter model (`COMFYUI_STARTER_MODEL_*`, pinned commit, SHA-256 and size checked, written as `.part` then renamed) unless `models/checkpoints` already has one. A plain `pip install -r` got the CPU-only build on Windows and no model, so a ComfyUI installed here could not make an image. Each card's `TOOL_META` entry says `usedBy` (which agent or page uses it; `null` = standalone) and `difference` (how it differs from similar tools), and `webUi` decides whether Open is shown (owner, 2026-10-22). Agents make images with ComfyUI only; Automatic1111, Fooocus, InvokeAI, Open WebUI and OpenHands are standalone. Since content factory phase 2a, Kokoro (voice-overs, `kokoro-onnx==0.6.1`, its model and voices downloaded at install from `KOKORO_MODEL_BASE` with SHA-256 and size checked) and AudioCraft (music beds) are used by Miya Studio, and Whisper also times the finished video's captions. Update `TOOL_META` when a tool gains or loses an in-app user |
| Prereq detection (Python/Git/Ollama) | `runtime_manager::find_python()` / `find_git()` / `find_ollama()` + `runtime_check_prerequisites` Tauri command |
| Prereq auto-install (winget/brew/apt) | `runtime_install_prerequisite` Tauri command — do not add a separate install flow |
| Bundled starter-model staging (Dependency Bundling Plan O2) | `scripts/fetch-starter-model.mjs` — reads `STARTER_MODEL_TAG` directly from `setupFlowService.ts`, fetches its manifest + blobs from Ollama's own registry API, stages into `src-tauri/vendor/starter-model/` in Ollama's real on-disk model-store layout (`blobs/sha256-<digest>`, `manifests/registry.ollama.ai/library/<name>/<tag>` — verified against a real local Ollama install's own store, not assumed). Self-verifying: each blob's filename is its own sha256 digest. Wired into `tauri.conf.json`'s `bundle.resources` (`"vendor/starter-model": "starter-model"`) and all 5 CI job sites across `ci.yml`/`release.yml` alongside the existing `fetch-ollama-runtime.mjs` step — adds a real ~1.9GB download per CI build, a deliberate, explicitly-approved tradeoff, not an oversight |
| Bundled starter-model boot-time loading | `bundled_starter_model_dir()` / `bundled_starter_model_blob_path()` / `runtime_load_bundled_starter_model` Tauri command, all in `runtime_manager.rs` — loads the bundled model via `ollama create <tag> -f <Modelfile>` (`FROM <local-blob-path>`) instead of a network pull, reusing the existing `run_streaming()` subprocess helper. Deliberately does NOT write into the user's `OLLAMA_MODELS` directory directly (see `detect_ollama_models_dir_free_gb()`'s doc comment for why this codebase refuses to guess that path) — `ollama create` lets Ollama itself resolve wherever it's actually configured to store models. Frontend: `loadBundledStarterModel()` in `runtimeManagerService.ts` (mirrors `installTool()`'s progress-listener pattern); `installComponent()` in `setupFlowService.ts` tries this first for the starter model, falling back to the existing `pullOllamaModel()` network path on any failure (no bundled resource in this build, or a genuine `ollama create` failure — same fallback for both, matching `isComponentAlreadyInstalled`'s established resilience posture). The underlying `ollama create -f Modelfile` technique was hand-verified for real (network-free, confirmed via `Get-NetTCPConnection`, real generated chat reply) before writing any Rust; the Tauri command itself has not yet been exercised end-to-end (needs a real Tauri window, blocked on this dev machine's Application Control policy) |
| Boot status banner | `src/components/BootStatusBanner.tsx` — listens to `runtime://boot_status` events |
| Ollama offline global banner | `src/components/OllamaOfflineBanner.tsx` — shown in App.tsx shell; Start/Retry/Open Runtime Hub; uses `startTool('ollama')` |
| Ollama preflight check panel | `src/components/OllamaPreflightPanel.tsx` — pings `checkOllama()`, records a `ollama.preflight` event via `eventsService.ts`, preselects `llama3.2:3b` if present; distinct from `OllamaOfflineBanner.tsx` (persistent global banner) — this is a one-shot inline check panel |
| First-run Setup flow ("Smart Installer") | `src/components/SetupFlow.tsx` — the step machine (boot → scan → intent → recommend → queue → activation/failed → launch) that **replaced `OnboardingWizard.tsx`** (removed from `src/` 2026-09-07, PR #233; source archived, not discarded — see the archive row below). Gated exactly where the wizard was: `useAppShellState.js`'s `showSetup` (seeded from `isSetupComplete()`) + a top-level conditional render in `App.tsx` that swaps out the whole app shell — NOT a second window, NOT a second binary, NOT Tauri's Splashscreen pattern (all three were considered and rejected; see the design doc §3 for why). Do NOT re-add an onboarding wizard — extend this instead. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-07-smart-installer-design.md`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-07-smart-installer.md` |
| Archived source — retired but kept for reference (not built, not imported) | `archive/MIGHTBEUSEDLATER/` — outside `src/`, so eslint/`verify-dnd-coverage`/Vite never see it; nothing here is part of the running app. Currently holds `OnboardingWizard.tsx` + its 2 test files (see `archive/MIGHTBEUSEDLATER/MIGHTBEUSEDLATER.md` for full context on why each entry was retired and what to check before ever resurrecting it — per `REPO_RULES.md` R14, don't restore anything from here into `src/` without direct approval, same as removing a file requires). Check this folder before rebuilding something that looks newly missing — it may already exist here, retired for a documented reason |
| Setup — boot ritual intro | `src/components/setup/BootRitualIntro.tsx` — the opening ceremony (design doc §5 step 1: "emblem forms, brief scanline sweep, skippable, 2-3s... Cyberpunk Ritual"), added 2026-09-08 after the original PR shipped only the *closing* half of the ritual (Activation Sequence) and skipped this opening half entirely. Reuses the exact same Framer Motion variants as `ActivationSequence.tsx` (`activationPulse`/`activationPortal`/`emblemIgnite` in `src/lib/motion.ts`) rather than inventing a second animation language, recolored to `--accent` cyan per the design doc's own resolved color split — only the closing Activation Sequence's emblem-reveal moment gets the real sampled green/orange, everything else in Setup (including this screen's neon grid lines, glow rings, and monospace HUD readout text) uses the app's existing cyan accent token. Skippable via click, Enter, Space, or Escape, auto-advances after 2.6s (300ms with `usePrefersReducedMotion()`); a `useRef` guard (not `useState`) prevents `onFinish` firing twice when the native `<button>`'s own Enter-activation and the document-level keydown listener both fire in the same tick. |
| Setup — system scan screen | `src/components/setup/SystemScan.tsx` — runs `scanHardware()` + `checkPrerequisites()` via `Promise.allSettled` (NOT `all` — a single probe failure must not discard the other's result) with `withTimeout()` on **both** call sites, so a non-Tauri host (plain browser: `npm run dev`, Playwright) degrades to "Unknown" instead of hanging forever on the scanning screen. Surfaces Docker-missing as manual-install-only (no auto-install path exists for Docker, unlike Python/Git/Ollama) |
| Setup — intent picker | `src/components/setup/IntentSelection.tsx` — 5 plain-language tiles (Chat Only / Chat + Images / Chat + Voice / Full Power Mode / Custom). Custom routes straight to the agent grid (see below), skipping the recommendation screen — there's no stated intent to combine with the hardware scan |
| Setup — recommendation screen | `src/components/setup/RecommendedSetup.tsx` — maps intent + hardware to a component list (`INTENT_RECOMMENDATIONS`, sizes verified against official sources in the design doc §6), shows hardware-fit warnings inline, marks already-installed components as satisfied via `getAllStatus()`, blocks on `checkDiskSpace()`, and excludes any component whose real prerequisite (`getUnmetPrereq`) isn't met — Python gets an inline "Install Python" button (a real auto-install path exists), Docker gets a manual-install note only (no auto-install path exists anywhere in this codebase). "Customize" routes to the agent grid below, not the deleted `RecommendableIntent`-typed 'custom' branch (the compiler enforces `RecommendedSetup` can never be asked to handle `'custom'` — see that type in the file) |
| Setup — agent grid (Custom path) | `src/components/setup/AgentGrid.tsx` — the power-user path both the Custom intent tile and RecommendedSetup's "Customize" button reach. Shows all 9 real agents (confirmed against `src/agents/*/*.js`, NOT the original draft's 8-tile roster which included "Boardroom" — a feature, not an agent — and omitted Sentinel/Nova). Only 4 have an installable component: Alphonso (starter model, always on, toggle disabled), Miya (ComfyUI with the starter image model; was Fooocus until 2026-10-22, which nothing in the app used), Marcus (Voice OS), Maria (ChromaDB); the other 5 (Jose/Hector/Echo/Sentinel/Nova) are software-only per this file's own agent rows and render as "included — no extra install needed" rather than a fake toggle. Reuses the same `checkDiskSpace`/`getUnmetPrereq`/`isComponentAlreadyInstalled` primitives as RecommendedSetup rather than a second implementation. Each tile shows a real agent portrait from `src/assets/agents/setup/*.webp` — cropped/compressed to ~5-16KB each (down from 5-6.6MB source PNGs) specifically so 9 of them don't meaningfully affect installer size; these are a **separate, Setup-specific asset set** from the pre-existing marketing mascots in `src/assets/*-mascot*.png` (full "info card" style with text overlays, used by `CompanionWidget.tsx`/`MarketingLandingPage.tsx`/`MiyaStudio.tsx`), not a duplicate of them — the marketing assets are unsuited to a small grid tile |
| Setup — install queue | `src/components/setup/InstallQueue.tsx` — installs every selected component in parallel via `installComponent()` (routes models vs. Runtime Hub tools to their real, different install mechanisms — see `setupFlowService.ts`'s row below), tracks per-task status, and calls `onFailed` (never `onAllComplete`) when any task errors — treating a failed queue as success would persist the setup-complete flag and permanently hide the retry path. Real per-task progress (2026-09-08): `installComponent()`'s optional `onProgress` callback normalizes `pullOllamaModel()`'s byte-based pull progress and `installTool()`'s real `runtime://progress` Tauri events into one `{message, pct}` shape, rendered as a percentage-width progress bar (indeterminate sweep when `pct` is `null`) colored via `AgentGrid.tsx`'s exported `COMPONENT_AGENT_COLORS` map — do not hardcode a second id-to-color table. The starter model reaching `ready` renders a pulsing `motion.button` ("Start Chatting Now") rather than auto-firing `onStarterReady()` — the design's "Early Exit → Chat Now" moment is a real user choice, not a silent jump; `onAllComplete()` is the fallback if the queue finishes successfully before that button is ever clicked |
| Setup — activation sequence | `src/components/setup/ActivationSequence.tsx` — `full` (3.5s, full-screen) vs `toast` (1.5s, non-blocking) variants. Real Framer Motion choreography: pulse (`activationPulse`) → portal expansion (`activationPortal`) → emblem ignite (`emblemIgnite`), all in `src/lib/motion.ts` alongside the app's other shared motion variants — timed to land inside the 3.5s full-variant window, not a separate animation system. Shows the real emblem (`src/assets/branding/alphonso-emblem.webp`, cropped/compressed from a 1024px master PNG — the master itself was a throwaway derivation input, removed from the repo after use, see the design doc's 2026-09-08 note). Uses `--emblem-green`/`--emblem-orange` (real brand colours sampled from the shipped app icon), scoped to this moment only — the app-wide `--accent` cyan is correct and deliberately unchanged. `usePrefersReducedMotion()` (see the hooks row below) skips the pulse/portal layers entirely and renders the emblem at its final state immediately rather than animating in — reduced motion means less animation, not less content, so the emblem and text still show |
| Setup state, disk precheck, completion flag, install routing | `src/services/setupFlowService.ts` — `scanHardware()`, pure `checkDiskSpace()` (a `null` free-space value means "couldn't measure" and does NOT block; only a real number below the threshold does; its optional `sameVolume` argument, from `sameInstallVolume(hardware)` over the scan's `diskMount`/`ollamaModelsDirMount`, merges the general and starter-model checks when both land on one drive and splits them when they don't, added 2026-10-21). The models folder comes from `configured_ollama_models_dir()` in `runtime_manager.rs`: `OLLAMA_MODELS` first, then the folder chosen in the Ollama desktop app's own Settings (`settings.models` in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Ollama\db.sqlite`, read-only, Windows only), which is also passed as `OLLAMA_MODELS` to an Ollama that Alphonso starts. Before 2026-10-21 a user who moved models in the Ollama app saw none of them in Alphonso's Ollama, and Setup re-downloaded the starter model onto C:, `withTimeout()`, `isSetupComplete()`/`markSetupComplete()` over `alphonso_setup_complete_v1` (migrates the legacy `alphonso_onboarding_complete_v1` flag forward so upgrading users aren't forced back through first-run), `STARTER_MODEL_ID`/`STARTER_MODEL_TAG` (single source of truth for the bundled default model's queue id and real ollama tag — reconcile with `DEPENDENCY_BUNDLING_PLAN.md`'s `O2` if it changes), `installComponent()`/`isComponentAlreadyInstalled()` (route the starter model through `ollama pull`/`/api/tags`, everything else through Runtime Hub's `installTool`/`getAllStatus` — passing a model id to `installTool` fails with "Unknown tool", which is exactly what shipped before this was fixed), and `getUnmetPrereq()` (pure lookup against a small `COMPONENT_PREREQ` map sourced from `runtime_manager.rs`'s `TOOLS` array — fooocus/voice-os need Python, chromadb needs Docker; an undefined prereq flag counts as unmet, never as "must be fine") |
| External URL opening (Tauri) | Use `invoke('open_url', { url })` — NOT bare `<a href target="_blank">` which fails silently in Tauri webview. A source link (a page Hector found) goes through `openSourceLink(url)` in `browserAutomationService.ts` (agent phase 4): it warns first when the link looks disguised or Google Web Risk lists it |

| Live install log streaming | `runtime://log` Tauri events + `onLogLine()` in runtimeManagerService + `LiveLogPanel` in RuntimeManagerView |

| Configured Ollama endpoint and model (for non-component code) | `getConfiguredOllamaEndpoint()` and `getConfiguredOllamaModel()` in `src/lib/ollama.ts` — read persisted `alphonso_settings.endpoint` / `.selectedModel` (the model falls back to `PREFERRED_MODEL`, which is the starter `llama3.2:3b` and must equal `STARTER_MODEL_TAG`; a test checks it). Do NOT pass `PREFERRED_MODEL` or `DEFAULT_OLLAMA_ENDPOINT` straight to a model call: until 2026-10-21 seven agent services did, asked for `qwen2.5-coder:7b`, and silently fell back to rules on every fresh install. Do NOT hardcode `http://localhost:11434` in a new service/component — that exact bug (6+ call sites ignoring the Settings endpoint field) was found and fixed 2026-08-22, see `docs/governance/DEFERRED_WORK.md`. React components that already receive `settings` as a prop should keep using `settings.endpoint` directly. `isChatModel(model)` (QA batch 0) tells an embedding model (`all-minilm`, `nomic-embed`, `bge`, `mxbai`, … or a `bert` family) from a chat model; `chooseDefaultModel()`, the Chat pickers, `listInstalledModels()` and Hector's fallback use it. Until then, once Echo's meaning search downloaded `all-minilm`, a missing chosen model fell back to it and every agent failed. Do NOT pick a chat model from the installed list without it. |
| Warning for a very small model (QA batch 1) | `src/components/SmallModelNotice.tsx` — `SmallModelNotice({ model, short })`, `isSmallModel()`, `SMALL_MODEL_MIN_B` (3), `SMALL_MODEL_NOTE(model)`: under 3B by the size in the model's name (`modelSize()` in `joseModels.ts`; no size, no warning), shown next to Chat's model picker (short, with the sentence as its tooltip), under Settings' Active Inference Model and under "Model for Alphonso's code". Blocks nothing (owner, 2026-10-28) |
| Random ids | `src/lib/randomId.ts` — `randomHex(length)` and `randomId(prefix)` (`<prefix>-<now>-<8 hex>`) from `crypto.getRandomValues` (phase 3d, CodeQL `js/insecure-randomness`: ids end up as session and request ids). `src/test/randomIds.test.js` fails if an id in `src` is made from `Math.random()` again; image seeds and shuffles may still use it. `secureId()` in `contentTypes.ts` and `resolveSecureSessionId()` in `hermesAgentConnector.ts` predate it. Do NOT make an id from `Math.random()` |

No keyword fallback, no switched-off message: an unreachable model fails as an unreadable review. Boardroom's Conclude shows her review as "Maria's governance review", never "(rule-based)" |
