_Last verified: 2026-07-10 (later same day) — Boardroom rebuild (12 phases) + PR #98 merge. User provided a bloome.im-style group chat handoff PDF and, after a Step 0/1 design review, explicitly authorized proceeding — overriding an earlier same-day "don't touch Boardroom yet" hold. Built via strict TDD across 12 independently-planned, independently-committed-and-pushed phases (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-10-boardroom-*-phase{1..12}.md`), each phase stating its own honest scope limitations up front rather than overclaiming: **Phase 1** — `boardroomThreadService.ts` (threads/messages, `@mention` parsing) + `BoardroomChatView.tsx` chat shell, replacing session-summary `BoardroomView.tsx`; legacy session migration. **Phase 2** — `@mention` autocomplete dropdown in the composer. **Phase 3** — real per-agent Ollama generation (`boardroomFacilitatorService.ts`), persona-specific system prompts sourced from `agentRegistry.js`, not hardcoded. **Phase 4** — multi-agent routing: `@mentioned` agents each generate a real reply; explicit architectural decision *not* to route through `agentBusService`/`a2aProtocolService` (those solve governed task delegation, not chat messaging). Live-verified against real Ollama mid-phase, which caught a real bug: `generateOllamaResponse`'s hardcoded 30s timeout failed against a real multi-model instance (cold model-swap alone took 47.3s) — bumped to 120s in `src/lib/ollama.ts`. **Phase 5** — bounded `@mention` chaining (a reply mentioning another agent triggers them too) capped at `MAX_CHAIN_DEPTH=3`, auto-escalating to an amber "Needs your decision" banner when hit — the phase explicitly flagged as the highest-risk one (uncontrolled generation loop) and scoped accordingly. **Phase 6** — cross-thread context recall (`findCrossThreadContext`) — plain keyword overlap across other threads, not semantic/embedding search. **Phase 7** — hedge-phrase low-confidence detection (`detectLowConfidence`) auto-escalates hedgy replies; stated plainly as a text-pattern heuristic since Ollama exposes no real confidence/logprob signal. **Phase 8** — Stop button halts further chained hops; does not abort an in-flight fetch (no `AbortController` wiring in `lib/ollama.ts`, shared by too many other callers to touch here). **Phase 9** — failed replies render distinct (rose) with Retry, reconstructing the call from a stored `retryContext`; Retry deliberately does not re-trigger chaining. **Phase 10** — one-way Acknowledge control on escalations only — explicitly not a multi-human "seen by" roster, since Boardroom has one human user. **Phase 11** — high-risk (`approvalRequired`) messages render masked behind a "Confirm to reveal" gate; grepped the entire Boardroom code path first and confirmed zero connector/execution calls exist there — this gates content exposure, not action execution, and says so. **Phase 12** — real measured model+latency label (`Date.now()` around the actual generation call) on successful replies only.

Then resolved the long-open PR #98 (`feat/in-app-auto-update`, OpenCode's work): merged `main` into the branch (25 commits, zero conflicts), then found and fixed the actual reason it was unmergeable — `package.json`/`package-lock.json` never declared `@tauri-apps/plugin-updater`/`@tauri-apps/plugin-process` despite `node_modules` having them installed and `UpdaterNotification.tsx` importing them; a fresh `npm ci` (what CI actually runs) would have failed the build. Rust-side plugin wiring (`Cargo.toml`, `lib.rs`, `capabilities/default.json`, `tauri.conf.json`) was already correct on `main`, contrary to an earlier same-session assumption that it was missing too — verified directly rather than re-assumed. Also fixed a second real, unrelated failure the merge surfaced: `Doc Count Freshness` CI check failing on 9 stale numeric doc claims (lib.rs line count drift, service/test file counts) across README.md/ARCHITECTURE.md/AGENTS.md — fixed against `scripts/shared/counters.mjs`'s actual computed values, not guessed, and `node scripts/verify-doc-counts.mjs` now passes clean.

Targeted test verification per phase (not batched): 95 tests passing across `boardroomThreadService.test.ts` (27), `boardroomFacilitatorService.test.ts` (15), `boardroomChatView.test.jsx` (25+), `appLazyImports.test.js` — plus 91/95/etc. intermediate counts at each phase checkpoint, all captured in the individual phase commits. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. `npm run lint` clean except one pre-existing unrelated `ChatView.tsx` console warning, not touched this session. package.json version: unchanged at 2.5.18 — this was a feature-build + branch-reconciliation pass, not a release. Commits this pass (main): `a632711`, `8a4cbfc` (Phase 4), `4ff93d2` (Phase 5), `78ef11e`, `cdca50c`, `bceb431` (Phase 6), `359d836`, `3ca8a34` (Phase 7), `73a6d86` (Phase 8), `357e6ff`, `f5bea52` (Phase 9), `7cc62b9`, `50d30a3` (Phase 10), `8af1b18`, `03610f4` (Phase 11), `6906af1`, `204b2a6` (Phase 12); plus on `feat/in-app-auto-update` → merged to `main`: `71036a7` (merge), `55fff06` (dependency fix), `cf069d5` (doc count fix). Still genuinely open: the merged auto-updater is code-complete but not yet live-verified against a real signed release (needs an actual version bump + tag); `companionIntegration.test.js`'s fabricated Tauri command name assertions (found, not yet fixed, pre-existing test-quality issue); the deliberately-deferred Boardroom spec items (cards, regenerate/diff view, resource contention, voice input, mobile parity).

_Last verified: 2026-07-10 — a user bug-report pass, not a sprint — the user reported 15 distinct issues (voice offline, CMD windows flashing, sidebar scroll clipping, auto-update not configured, Telegram completely dead, Coach Mode not opening, Docker/Runway confusion, Boardroom scroll + purpose confusion + "Hermes" mystery + an unexplained approval gate, Miya's role unclear, Orchestration page bloat, mobile companion P0 pairing failure, and a generalized "every connector is broken" concern) and asked for root causes, fixes, and doc updates — not a version bump, no release cut this pass. Every fix below was verified against real code, not assumptions: read the actual invoke() calls against actual registered Tauri commands, read the actual test suite contracts before rewriting `pipeline.py`, ran the actual test suites before and after every change.

Fixed, in order: (1) Telegram — `telegramCompanionService.js` called nonexistent Tauri commands (`telegram_get_updates`/`telegram_send_message` vs. the real `connector_poll_telegram`/`connector_send_telegram`), silently swallowed by a bare `catch{}`; also fixed a `return`-vs-`continue` bug in the inbound message loop that dropped every message after the first in a poll batch, and a dead second `/memory` branch that meant `/memory <query>` never searched. (2) CMD windows flashing — `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` wasn't applied at several `Command::new()` sites across `lib.rs`/`plugin_runtime.rs`/`voice_sidecar.rs`/`workspace.rs`/`runtime_manager.rs`, including the polled `kill_pid`/`is_pid_alive` health checks (most likely source of *recurring* flashes); added a shared `no_window()` helper in `utils.rs` and applied it everywhere. (3) Sidebar nav scroll clipping — nav item list was `shrink-0` inside an `overflow-hidden` parent with no scroll of its own. (4) Coach Mode completely non-functional — the Coach webview window had zero Tauri capability grants (`capabilities/default.json` scoped everything to `"windows": ["main"]`, no `"coach"` entry) so even when the window opened nothing inside it could work; also fixed `openCoachWindow()` to actually await creation success/failure instead of resolving optimistically, and fixed 3 more call sites in `CoachContext.jsx` with the same silent-catch bug as the one initially patched (found and fixed after the user asked what bugs were found-but-not-reported). (5) Voice OS completely non-functional — root cause was far worse than a config gap: `voice/backend/pipeline.py` called a nonexistent LLM function with the conversation-history list passed as if it were callable (crashed every request after transcription), never `await`ed the async `synthesize()` TTS call, and had no VAD gate at all; fully rewritten against the pre-existing `tests/test_pipeline.py` contract with a real Ollama `/api/chat` streaming call added. (6) Mobile Companion pairing P0 — the actual root cause: port 8765 was double-booked between the Companion WebSocket server and Voice OS (with a second, independent Voice OS launch path in `runtime_manager.rs`'s ToolDef catalogue also on 8765). Whichever started first won the bind silently; the other failed with zero UI feedback. iOS hardcodes 8765 in Swift, so Voice OS was moved to 8766 across 6 files instead. (7) WhatsApp — built real command handling from scratch (`whatsappCompanionService.ts`, 9 commands mirroring Telegram's pattern, all calling real underlying services) since none existed; wired auto-start at boot (previously only a manual "Poll" button existed); found and fixed a second real bug while wiring it — `WHATSAPP_CLOUD_GATEWAY_DRAIN_URL`, required for inbound polling to function at all, had no UI field anywhere in the app. (8) `voice/backend/router.py` keyword-routing bugs — hector's broad `find`/`scan` keywords stole matches meant for nova/sentinel due to dict check order; miya's pattern was missing write/blog/draft/copy/email keywords entirely; 31/31 pytest passing (was 28/31). (9) `UpdaterNotification.tsx`'s button relabeled from "Update & Restart" (misleading — it never restarted anything) to "Download Update". (10) `BOARDROOM_ROLES.md`/`BOARDROOM_MODEL_REGISTRY.md` corrected with banners — both describe an early 11-seat aspirational design (including "Hermes," "Kairo," and "Shayan" as a boardroom seat) that was never actually built; the real product only has the 9-agent roster.

Explanatory findings, not fixes (user asked "explain, don't build yet"): Boardroom's actual purpose/workflow and honest limitations; the approval-confirmation gate in `BoardroomView.tsx` is real and functional but client-side only; Miya is architected as the creative-direction/planning layer, not the raw generation engine (confirmed directly from her profile's own stated limitations); the Orchestration page (`OrchestratorView.tsx`) is not dead scaffolding — every panel calls real services — but has genuine duplication between its embedded `OrchestratorQueueView` and a separate hand-rolled Monitor-tab panel covering the same data on a different refresh cadence (left alone per explicit user instruction, not yet fixed); Docker has zero code linkage to the Runway connector anywhere in the codebase (only n8n requires Docker) — likely a UI-adjacency confusion, not a bug.

Handed off, not built: full in-app auto-update (download+install+relaunch) — `docs/AUTO_UPDATE_HANDOFF.md` + PR #98 on branch `feat/in-app-auto-update`, deliberately not attempted blind since it needs new Tauri plugins and can only be properly verified against a real signed release.

Explicitly declined: a large external Boardroom-rebuild spec (bloome.im-style group chat UX, @mentions, agent critique/pushback, 20-step implementation plan) arrived as a handoff PDF mid-session — flagged to the user that it conflicts with their own earlier "don't touch Boardroom yet" instruction and was not acted on pending explicit sign-off.

Previous entry (v2.5.10, 2026-07-02) — First real release cut since v2.4.4, requested by the user (tag + CI release + installer). Tagged and pushed `v2.5.9`; `.github/workflows/release.yml` built and published successfully (19m47s). Checked the published release rather than assuming success meant correctness: `gh release view v2.5.9` showed the installer asset named `Alphonso_2.4.4_x64-setup.exe`, not `2.5.9`. Root cause: `package.json`'s version had been bumped every sprint (2.4.4 → 2.5.9) but `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` and `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` — the actual Tauri app version driving the installer filename, in-app About display, and updater version comparisons — were never touched, silently stuck at `2.4.4` through 9 version bumps (only surfaced now because no release had been cut in between). Fixed both files plus `Cargo.lock`'s `app` entry, bumped `package.json` to match, verified with `cargo check`, and cut a corrected release under `v2.5.10` rather than force-moving the already-public `v2.5.9` tag. Process fix for future sprints: bumping `package.json`'s version must also bump the 3 Tauri-side version locations in the same commit — added to `ALPHONSOTOTHEMOON.md`'s process notes. package.json / tauri.conf.json / Cargo.toml version: 2.6.5 (all three in sync). Previous entry (v2.5.6, 2026-07-02) — ALPHONSOTOTHEMOON Sprint 4 (security hardening Batch 2): audited already-merged Sprint 2 surfaces directly (the diff-scoped `security-review` skill doesn't apply when there's no pending PR). Found and fixed a real authentication-bypass bug: `telegramCompanionService.js`'s `/start` owner registration was first-come-first-served — whichever chat messaged first became the *permanent* owner with full command authority over Jose, exploitable because Telegram bot usernames (unlike tokens) are publicly searchable. Fixed by gating registration on `TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_CHAT_IDS`, a credential field that already existed in `ConnectorSetupPanel.tsx` but was never enforced — reused it instead of building a new pairing-code mechanism (discovered mid-implementation, documented the pivot). Also hardened constant-time token comparison on both `gateway/generic-webhook` and `gateway/whatsapp-cloud` (the WhatsApp gateway's HMAC signature check already did this correctly; only the simpler bearer/query-token checks had drifted to plain `===`). Traced Discord/generic-webhook/WhatsApp inbound paths and confirmed none of them auto-forward content into agent prompts except Telegram (the one now fixed). Confirmed `npm audit`/`cargo audit` already gate CI (no `continue-on-error`). Credential storage (OS-level secret storage vs. localStorage/SQLite) documented as a Sprint 6 recommendation per the user's explicit choice not to implement it this sprint. Bonus: fixed the `ConnectorSetupPanel.test.jsx` mock gap open since Sprint 2 while already touching that file. Targeted tests: 48/48 passing (`telegramCompanionService` 22, `ConnectorSetupPanel` 7, `whatsappCloudGateway`, `whatsappGatewaySecurity`, `genericWebhookService`); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean. package.json version: 2.6.5. All docs updated same-pass. Previous entry (v2.5.5, 2026-07-02) — ALPHONSOTOTHEMOON Sprint 3 (discoverability-audit half, closing out Sprint 3): drove the actual running app with Playwright (headless Chromium against `npm run dev`) rather than only reading source, per the sprint's own requirement that exposure — not just wiring — needed a real click-through pass. Found and fixed a critical bug this way: opening Sidebar → Boardroom → "Boardroom Sessions" crashed the entire app with an uncaught `TypeError` inside React's own lazy-loading path, because `App.tsx`'s `lazy(() => import('./components/BoardroomView'))` was missing the `.then((mod) => ({ default: mod.BoardroomView }))` mapping that all 25 other lazy-loaded views in that file use — `BoardroomView.tsx` only has a named export, so React.lazy resolved `undefined` as the component type. Fixed and verified live (renders correctly, zero console errors); added two regression tests (`boardroomView.test.jsx`, `appLazyImports.test.js` — the latter statically checks all 26 lazy imports in `App.tsx`, confirming this was the only mismatch). Confirmed live: Coach Mode is real/functional (not a bug, just visually understated next to Settings/Theme); Agent Pairing and Ecosystem Maturity/Self-Development panels are real and render correctly but sit 2 clicks deep behind generic tab labels; Operator Dashboard has no sidebar entry at all — reachable only via a Dashboard quick-launch card, and shows nothing but a bare "Enable" gate when Operator Mode is off. No further code changes made for these UI-placement findings this pass — logged as a follow-up UX decision, not silently fixed or silently dropped. package.json version: 2.6.5. Targeted tests: 46/46 passing across `boardroomView`, `appLazyImports`, `ecosystemHub`, `agentPairingView`, `selfDevelopmentService`; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. All docs updated same-pass.
